# Proposed OEIS comments: A221162 to A224200

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A221162

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A221162>  
Result obtained: **3 September 2026**  
Paper: [991](../papers/0501-1000/0991-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 13*a(n-1) - 26*a(n-2) + a(n-3).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(3 - 6*x + x^2) / (1 - 13*x + 26*x^2 - x^3). - _Colin Barker_, Aug 04 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 167 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 3. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A221374

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A221374>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [8606](../papers/8501-9000/8606-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 9*a(n-1) - 12*a(n-2) + 4*a(n-3).
> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Aug 05 2018: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence follows from the generating function this entry already states as fact: a recurrence with constant coefficients is exactly a denominator of the generating function, so the two are the same statement, and it holds for n > 3. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Paper: [784](../papers/0501-1000/0784-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 4*a(n-1) + 8*a(n-3) - 32*a(n-4) - 16*a(n-6) + 64*a(n-7).
> Empirical g.f.: 2*x*(1 + 4*x - 20*x^3 + 16*x^4 + 32*x^6) / ((1 - 4*x)*(1 - 4*x^3)^2). - _Colin Barker_, Mar 15 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. ```

## A223211

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A223211>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [3294](../papers/3001-3500/3294-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-1) + 4*a(n-2) = 6*A063782(n-1).
> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Aug 17 2018: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 6 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Result obtained: **3 September 2026**  
Paper: [3043](../papers/3001-3500/3043-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 1600*a(n-1) - 368640*a(n-2) + 21233664*a(n-3).
> Empirical g.f.: 1024*x*(1 - 304*x + 18432*x^2) / (1 - 1600*x + 368640*x^2 - 21233664*x^3). - _Colin Barker_, Aug 19 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 6 145 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 3. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A223444>  
Result obtained: **31 August 2026**  
Paper: [6565](../papers/6501-7000/6565-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 70*a(n-2) -1024*a(n-4) +2832*a(n-6) -1792*a(n-8) +128*a(n-10)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 3 375 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 10. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A223719>  
Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [2098](../papers/2001-2500/2098-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = (1/576)*n^8 + (1/48)*n^7 + (41/288)*n^6 + (13/24)*n^5 + (793/576)*n^4 + (31/16)*n^3 + (119/48)*n^2 + (3/2)*n + 1.
> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Feb 19 2018: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical closed form is correct. The count is a walk count in a finite graph, so it satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence; the conjectured form satisfies the same one, and the two agree at enough consecutive indices to agree from then on, for n > 9. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. > Empirical: a(n) = 4*a(n-1) - 2*a(n-2) - a(n-3) - 4*a(n-4) + 14*a(n-5) - 2*a(n-6) - 2*a(n-9).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(7 - 9*x^2 - 12*x^3 + 10*x^4 - 2*x^8) / (1 - 4*x + 2*x^2 + x^3 + 4*x^4 - 14*x^5 + 2*x^6 + 2*x^9). - _Colin Barker_, Aug 28 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 56 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 9. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```
